[Director 指令] [Patch — T_ext sensitivity appendix table]

T_ext 消融实验完成（Mistral-7B FOLIO-204），加入 appendix 作为参数敏感性分析。

**数据：**

| T_ext | SICA Acc (%) | Δ vs SC (pp) | Avg #Constraints |
|-------|-------------|-------------|-----------------|
| 0.1 | 57.35 | +1.47 | 69.3 |
| **0.3** | **59.31** | **+3.43** | **68.8** |
| 0.5 | 56.37 | +0.49 | 68.2 |
| 0.7 | 53.43 | -2.45 | 67.3 |
| 1.0 | 55.39 | -0.49 | 65.2 |

SC baseline = 55.88%。T_ext=0.3 是论文默认值。

**放置位置**：appendix，在 TOST 节之后或 single-trace stats 附近。

**叙事要点（1-2 句）**：
- T_ext=0.3 产生最高 SICA accuracy（59.31%），呈倒 U 型：过低温过于 deterministic，过高温导致约束发散质量下降
- 验证论文默认参数选择合理

标题建议："\paragraph{Extraction Temperature Sensitivity}" 或 "\subsection{T_ext Sensitivity}"

_source line 17240 · 2026-05-21T06:47:08.468Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch — T_ext appendix 叙事修正]

Reviewer 指出 T_ext=0.3 的 SC baseline (54.41%) 与其他条件 (55.88%) 不同（不同 seed/trace set），表面的"inverted U-shape, 0.3 最优"是混淆变量。

**修正后结论**：控制 seed 后（T_ext=0.1/0.5/0.7/1.0 四个条件，同一 SC=55.88%），最大 Δ 仅 +1.47pp (n.s.)，T_ext 对 SICA 无显著影响。

**修改 appendix T_ext paragraph**：
- 删除"inverted U-shape"和"T_ext=0.3 is optimal"的表述
- 改为：T_ext 在 0.1–1.0 范围内对 SICA accuracy 无显著影响（控制 trace set 后，所有 Δ ≤ +1.47pp, all n.s.），表明 symbolic confirmation bias 是结构性的，不依赖特定提取温度
- 保留表格数据，但加脚注说明 T_ext=0.3 使用了不同 trace set（SC=54.41%），其余使用统一 traces（SC=55.88%）
- T_ext=0.3 行不再加粗

这个修正后的结论实际上更强地支撑论文 story：confirmation bias 是 inherent，不是参数调优能解决的。

R10 审稿 3.0 Findings。以下是定稿的修改清单，按优先级分批执行。DDL 5/25。

---

## 批次 1：Critical 降级（快速，不需新内容）

### C1. Proposition 1 + Corollary 1 降级
- method.tex: `\begin{proposition}` → 改为 `\paragraph{Empirical Bound 1 (Self-Extraction Ceiling).}` 或类似非 theorem 环境
- method.tex: `\begin{corollary}` → 改为 `\paragraph{Empirical Observation 2 (Capability Gap Condition).}`
- method.tex: "Proof sketch" → "Empirical support"
- 全文引用 "Proposition 1" → "Bound 1"，"Corollary 1" → "Observation 2"

### C2. Abstract K_eff ordinal 化
- Abstract 中 "K_eff ≈ 1.20–3.62" → "K_eff reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics across generators"（删掉具体数值区间）
- method.tex 已有完整 ordinal caveat (lines 21-23)，不需改

### C3. Spearman 措辞降级
- 全文 "perfectly monotonically" → "strongly monotonically"
- 保持 n=6 为 primary（PW, all w=3, clean design）
- 加 1 句："Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces statistical power, making the test conservative."
- n=9 (ρ=0.836, p=0.007) 保持在 appendix 作 robustness check

---

## 批次 2：Critical 新内容（最高优先级 — 3.0→3.5 杠杆）

### C4. 新颖性论证（四层 argument，§1 + §2）

**§1 加 1-2 句定位**（在 "27 strategies" 之后）：
"Unlike prior work on self-correction failure in natural language (Huang et al., 2024; Stechly et al., 2025), we test a stronger condition: whether formalization into first-order logic with a provably sound solver can break the self-reinforcement loop."

**§2 Related Work neurosymbolic 段，加 1 段四层论证**：

Layer 1 (社区隐含假设):
"The neurosymbolic reasoning community operates under an implicit assumption that formalization provides reliability—converting natural language reasoning into formal logic should allow sound solvers (SAT/SMT) to compensate for LLM unreliability. Works such as SatLM, Logic-LM, and LINC have reported positive results under this assumption."

Layer 2 (本文挑战):
"We test this assumption by extracting FOL constraints from CoT traces and solving them with Z3 MAX-SAT—a solver with completeness guarantees. Our finding that 83.3% of constraints on incorrectly-answered questions support the wrong majority answer reveals that formalization guarantees are vacuous when the constraints themselves are contaminated."

Layer 3 (vs Huang/Stechly — 锐化版):
"Huang et al. (2024) and Stechly et al. (2025) show that LLM self-correction fails in generation→evaluation feedback loops. We advance this finding: even replacing evaluation with a provably sound formal solver (Z3), bias enters at the earlier generation→extraction stage. This shifts the bottleneck from evaluation quality to extraction quality—a more fundamental limitation, since Z3's completeness guarantees cannot compensate for upstream confirmation bias in constraint extraction."

Layer 4 (SatLM 连接):
"SatLM (Ye et al., 2023) provides a natural control: it generates formal specifications directly from problem descriptions, bypassing CoT traces entirely. Its positive results confirm that formal solvers are effective when fed uncontaminated constraints. The contrast between SatLM's success and SICA's failure precisely isolates the contamination pathway: the bottleneck is not the solver but what feeds it."

### C5. 三范式分类（融入 §2 neurosymbolic 段）

在 C4 Layer 4 之后，加分类总结：

"We identify three paradigms in neurosymbolic LLM reasoning:
(i) *Specification-generation* (SatLM, Logic-LM): LLM generates formal specifications from problem descriptions without CoT traces—no answer contamination;
(ii) *Program-generation* (PAL, Program of Thoughts): LLM generates executable code, with execution results as answers—similarly uncontaminated;
(iii) *Trace-extraction* (this work): constraints are extracted from existing CoT traces that already contain candidate answers—structurally contaminated.
Hybrid approaches that generate verification steps (e.g., VeriCoT) face intermediate contamination risk."

---

## 批次 3：Warning 修复

### W1. DRC caveat
- §6.1 加 1 句："We note that τ was selected on the evaluation set; held-out validation across datasets is needed before deployment."

### W2. Cross-arch escape framing
- §6 正文加 2-3 句："Cross-architecture NLI verification is effective under specific conditions: (i) premise-conclusion decomposable tasks, (ii) positive capability gap (verifier accuracy exceeds generator SC), and (iii) sufficient NLI training coverage. It did not improve results on LogiQA, where premise-conclusion boundaries are less clear."

### W3. Power limitation
- §4 结果讨论加 1 句："Observed statistical power for confirmatory conditions remains below 21%, reflecting the small per-condition effect sizes; our conclusions rest primarily on the convergent evidence across 16 conditions rather than any single test."

### W4. TOST margin
- 加 1-2 句：TOST margin ±2pp 选择理由——SC 文献典型改进 2-5pp，±2pp 作为 SESOI (Lakens, 2017) 保守下界。Agent 正在计算 ±1pp/±3pp sensitivity，完成后追加 appendix。

### W5. BR CI
- Agent 正在计算 bootstrap CI。完成后你把 CI 加入 §5.2 正文（如 "BR = 4.97× [95% CI: X–Y]"）。

### W6. SCB vs CoT confirmation bias
- §2 或 §5 加 1 段区分（与 C4 Layer 2 自然连接）：
"Recent work has identified confirmation bias in CoT reasoning (Chen et al., 2025; Liu et al., 2026). Our 'Symbolic Confirmation Bias' identifies a specific mechanism: bias persists even after cross-modal formalization (NL→FOL) and sound verification (Z3 MAX-SAT), demonstrating that the bias is structural rather than an artifact of natural language evaluation."

### W7. Related Work
- 与 C5 合并（三范式分类扩展 neurosymbolic 段）
- SC 段补 ToT (Yao et al., 2023) 引用

### W8. 27 策略正文展示
- §5.4 加 1 段分类（3-4 句）：
"The 27 strategies span four categories: (i) prompt-level (contrastive prompting, ICL with gold constraints), (ii) method-level (premise decoupling, bidirectional verification), (iii) solver-level (penalty weight tuning, soft constraints), and (iv) diversity-level (temperature, top-p variation). Representative results: contrastive prompting reduces BR from 4.97× to 3.82× but does not reach significance; premise decoupling eliminates answer leakage but constraints become too weak to shift votes."

55	
56	\begin{proposition}[Self-Extraction Ceiling]\label{prop:ceiling}
57	For generator $M$ with $\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\Delta_\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{self} \lesssim \Delta_\text{oracle} / \text{BR}(M)$.
58	\end{proposition}
59	
60	\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition]\label{cor:capability_gap}
61	Let $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.
62	\end{corollary}
63	
64	\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\ref{prop:ceiling}, adding $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\text{eff}^\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases perfectly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). $\square$
65	
```

### Tool result

_source line 17270 · 2026-05-21T07:00:45.801Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LFUiXEUgsizgGH2yKmvt1z`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	Cross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain decreasing perfectly monotonically as the capability gap narrows (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).
9	We further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at 39\% of verification cost.
10	
```

</details>

Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\n\\textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\\rho$) in \\citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). If traces are unfaithful \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Self-Extraction Ceiling]\\label{prop:ceiling}\nFor generator $M$ with $\\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\\Delta_\\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\\Delta_\\text{max}^\\text{self} \\lesssim \\Delta_\\text{oracle} / \\text{BR}(M)$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition]\\label{cor:capability_gap}\nLet $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\\Delta_\\text{combo} - \\Delta_\\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\\text{acc} - \\text{SC}_G)$.\n\\end{corollary}\n\n\\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling}, adding $V$ with $\\rho_{GV} < \\rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\\text{eff}^\\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\\text{acc} < \\text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases perfectly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). $\\square$\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}\nThese findings illuminate why trained reasoning internalization (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves $86\\%$ on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\\sim}62\\%$; Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling}). Rather than extracting symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck entirely.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

{

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/solutions.tex",
    "oldString": "Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}",
    "newString": "Observation~2",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases perfectly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}).\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01GfgCT3gBp82StDNt62yxTu`</summary>

```text
715	Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli} reports the controlled cross-generator comparison with DeBERTa-large-mnli on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$). We extend this analysis by pairing Mistral-7B with two additional NLI architectures: BART-large-mnli (46.17\% standalone, combo $+5.17$\,pp, $p = 0.0002$) and RoBERTa-large-mnli (37.67\% standalone, combo $+4.17$\,pp, $p = 0.003$). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains on Mistral-7B at $w \geq 3$.
716	
717	Across all six ProofWriter verifier-generator pairs at $w{=}3$ (three verifiers $\times$ Mistral-7B plus three negative-gap generators $\times$ DeBERTa), combo gain decreases perfectly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n = 6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}). Only conditions where the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC accuracy show significant improvement, confirming Corollary~\ref{cor:capability_gap}. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) survive.
718	
719	\paragraph{Robustness check.} Including all nine conditions (three verifiers $\times$ three weight levels on Mistral-7B) yields Spearman $\rho = 0.836$ (exact permutation $p = 0.007$, $n = 9$), confirming that the monotonic trend is robust to the choice of weight parameter.
720	
721	\paragraph{Full NLI combo with BH correction.}
722	\label{app:nli-combo-full}
```

</details>

    "newString": "yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a perfectly monotonic capability-gap trend ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

    "oldString": "perfectly monotonically",
    "newString": "strongly monotonically",
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain decreasing perfectly monotonically as the capability gap narrows (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).\nWe further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at 39\\% of verification cost.\n",

    "oldString": "perfectly monotonically",
    "newString": "strongly monotonically",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \\emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \\emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases perfectly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",

      {
        "oldStart": 12,
        "oldLines": 4,
        "newStart": 12,
        "newLines": 4,
        "lines": [

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a perfectly monotonic capability-gap trend ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline.     "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases perfectly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2).\n\n",

14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator.
16

In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). ```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01JXDSznwdKooKS2q3P8aNAB`</summary>

```text
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	Across six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.
25	Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
```

     \paragraph{Empirical Bound 1 (Self-Extraction Ceiling).}\label{prop:ceiling}
     For generator $M$ with $\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\Delta_\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{self} \lesssim \Delta_\text{oracle} / \text{BR}(M)$.

     \paragraph{Empirical Observation 2 (Capability Gap Condition).}\label{cor:capability_gap}
     Let $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.

     \textit{Empirical support.} From Bound~1, adding $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases $K_\text{eff}^\text{combo}$, but incorrect verifier votes counteract gains when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$. Empirically, combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
     ```

Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 90\% of this gain at 39\% cost.

        "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2).\n\n",

        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \\emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \\emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",
        "numLines": 15,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 15
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "bd7e42ce-123e-4028-8aba-0894386b92f1",
  "isSidechain": false,

    "type": "file",
