    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a strongly monotonic capability-gap trend ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",
        "numLines": 80,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 80
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "1061914f-ee1c-409e-98ca-dc0012447558",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nCross-architecture NLI verification yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator (Mistral-7B $+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$, $w{=}3$), with combo gain decreasing strongly monotonically as the capability gap narrows (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).\nWe further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at 39\\% of verification cost.\n",
        "numLines": 10,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 10
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/abstract.tex"
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016rAZtZ9mqGdmpQVyk9E8QW`</summary>

```text
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	Across six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.
25	Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp); other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	
31	Our contributions are:
32	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
33	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
34	
35	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a strongly monotonic capability-gap trend ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
36	
37	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
38	
39	\item \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\% of verification cost (\S\ref{sec:drc}).
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015i11Dv4RQuMQGkiHqcD3pn`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors. SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).
```

  "new_string": "Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction; neurosymbolic methods implicitly assume that formalizing into logic provides a reliable verification channel.\nWe test the strongest version of this assumption: first-order logic checked by a provably sound SMT solver (Z3), which \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints.\nThat this stronger channel still fails is the central surprise: Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed)."

    "oldString": "Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).",
    "newString": "Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction; neurosymbolic methods implicitly assume that formalizing into logic provides a reliable verification channel.\nWe test the strongest version of this assumption: first-order logic checked by a provably sound SMT solver (Z3), which \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints.\nThat this stronger channel still fails is the central surprise: Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that reveals near-deterministic voting dynamics ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2$--$3.6$ for nominal $K = 12$; should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic rather than a literal effective sample size, \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.46$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nAcross six models (7B--32B), three domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication, twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nPrior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).\n\nOracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling.\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage: DeBERTa NLI yields $+6.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC---even under formal Z3-checked verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three families; twenty-seven strategies fail. Cross-architecture NLI yields a significant gain when the verifier exceeds the generator ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$; $w{=}3$), with a strongly monotonic capability-gap trend ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$) across four models from three families; process homogeneity persists regardless of architecture (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC).} A Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, recovering 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain at 39\\% of verification cost (\\S\\ref{sec:drc}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,\n                text width=1.2cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract logical\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n",

          "+Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction; neurosymbolic methods implicitly assume that formalizing into logic provides a reliable verification channel.",
          "+We test the strongest version of this assumption: first-order logic checked by a provably sound SMT solver (Z3), which \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints.",
          "+That this stronger channel still fails is the central surprise: Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because they are individually valid yet collectively biased---the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).",

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \\emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \\emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} each introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle})."
}

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} and LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc} translate problems into formal logic for external provers; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors. SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm} generates complete SAT formulations from problem descriptions and reports positive results; critically, SatLM's constraints derive from \\emph{problem-level} understanding, whereas SICA extracts from \\emph{reasoning traces}. Problem-level formalization avoids answer contamination; trace-level extraction inherits confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Program-aided approaches \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} parallel the self-extraction bottleneck. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",

    "newString": "Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \\citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}\nSC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \\citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \\citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \\citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms. \\emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\\sim}$40\\% translation errors even at this level. \\emph{Program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}\nScaling test-time computation through additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \\citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to the independence bottleneck: QwQ-32B produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \\citep{weng2023selfverification}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",

We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.\n\n\\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}\nCJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.\nPrior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 12,
        "oldLines": 4,
        "newStart": 12,
        "newLines": 4,
        "lines": [
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}",
          " CJT \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification.",
          "-Prior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization, connecting to CoT faithfulness concerns \\citep{turpin2024language}; conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",
          "\\ No newline at end of file",
          "+Prior self-correction studies \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics. Effective-$K$ (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) measures independent information across $K$ traces; the bias ratio (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:mirage}) quantifies post-hoc rationalization. Symbolic confirmation bias is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language}: the latter concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution regardless of logical form. Conclusion leakage rate ($C_D$) isolates step-level contamination. Together, these show that formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction. The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides a practical decision criterion: combo gain decreases strongly monotonically with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$).",

1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Encoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	We evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
20	\vspace{-3mm}
21	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}
22	\label{fig:capability-gap}
23	\vspace{-6mm}
24	\end{figure}
25	
26	\begin{table}[t]
27	\centering
28	\small
29	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
30	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
31	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
32	\toprule
33	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
34	\midrule
35	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
36	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
37	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
38	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
39	\bottomrule
40	\end{tabular}}
41	\end{table}
42	
43	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
44	\label{sec:drc}
45	
46	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
47	
48	\begin{table}[t]
49	\centering
50	\small
51	\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
52	\label{tab:drc}
53	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
54	\toprule
55	Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
56	\midrule
57	SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
58	DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
59	\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
60	DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
61	Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
62	\bottomrule
63	\end{tabular}
64	\end{table}
65	
66	DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\,pp out of $+6.67$\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2).
67

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	
16	\subsection{Main Results}
17	\label{sec:main-results}
18	
19	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.
20	
21	\begin{table*}[t]
22	\centering
23	\small
24	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (55.88\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$);\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$ (oracle). Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}. $^{4s}$\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.}
25	\label{tab:main_results}
26	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
27	\toprule
28	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
29	\midrule
30	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ 40\%)}} \\
31	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\
32	\midrule
33	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\sim$50--55\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\
34	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
35	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\
36	Mistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\
37	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
38	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
39	\midrule
40	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\geq$ 60\%)}} \\
41	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\
42	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
43	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
44	Qwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\
45	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\
46	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\
47	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\
48	Qwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
49	Qwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\
50	Qwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\
51	\midrule
52	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\%$)}} \\
53	QwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
54	Qwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
55	\midrule
56	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\
57	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \textbf{85.78} & $\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\times}10^{-13}${***} \\
58	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \textbf{89.71} & $\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\times}10^{-7}${***} \\
59	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \textbf{86.67} & $\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\times}10^{-15}${***} \\
60	\bottomrule
61	\end{tabular}
62	\end{table*}
63	
64	No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) confirm 5 equivalent, 5 genuinely different (4 negative), 6 underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure below.
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Oracle Control}
68	\label{sec:oracle}
69	
70	Replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\Delta$ ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling.
71	
72	
73

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
15	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
16	\label{tab:independence}
17	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
18	\toprule
19	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
20	\midrule
21	Qwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\sim}$84 & ${\sim}$0 \\
22	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\
23	Qwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\
24	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\
25	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\
26	\addlinespace
27	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\
28	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\
29	Mistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\
30	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\
31	Mistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\
32	Mistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\
33	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\
34	Mistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\
35	Mistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\
36	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\
37	\bottomrule
38	\end{tabular}
39	\end{table}
40	
41	
42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	Bias generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting; bias intensifies along reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 3 \times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}).
48	
49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.
59	
60	
61	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
62	\label{sec:diagnostics}
63	
64	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
65	
66	
67	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
68	\label{sec:remediation}
69	\label{sec:interventions}
70	
71	Twenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
72	
73	\begin{figure}[t]
74	\centering
75	\includegraphics[width=0.85\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
76	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
77	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
78	\end{figure}

SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nSelf-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\nBias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting; bias intensifies along reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 3 \\times 10^{-6}$; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nTwenty-seven remediation strategies all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by category. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}",

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.
9	
10	\paragraph{Implications for trained reasoning.}
11	These findings illuminate why trained reasoning internalization (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains: Qwen3-14B thinking achieves $86\%$ on FOLIO, exceeding Mistral-7B's self-extraction ceiling (${\sim}62\%$; Bound~1). Rather than extracting symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck entirely.
12	
13	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
14	Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap ($\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
15	
```

```text
1	\paragraph{Scale and language.}
2	All models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. The cross-architecture NLI verifier (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo $\Delta \leq +1$\,pp, all $p > 0.5$), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment---NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment. Even on compatible tasks, NLI gain vanishes when the capability gap is large (Qwen3-14B ProofWriter: SC 85.83\% vs.\ DeBERTa 55\%, gap $= -30.83$\,pp, $\Delta \leq +0.50$\,pp, n.s.), and moderate negative gaps yield small, non-significant gains (LLaMA-3.1-8B: gap $= -17.50$\,pp, $+0.83$\,pp, n.s.; \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Scaling the cross-architecture approach---via trained verifiers, code-based symbolic execution, or multi-verifier ensembles---remains unexplored and may extend the escape to stronger generators and broader task types.
3	
4	\paragraph{Statistical power.}
5	The per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \textsc{False} problems, 69 \textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\approx$ 50--55\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}.
6	
7	\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}
8	SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\ 17.7\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\% of overhead (\S\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.
9	
10	\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.
11	
```
