    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$).     "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nEncoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). Per-label complementarity emerges: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nWe evaluate four generators from three model families spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}). Mistral-7B---the only generator whose SC accuracy (39.33\\%) falls below the verifier's standalone accuracy---gains $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), while LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$\\,pp), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$\\,pp), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no significant improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ and $w{=}5$ survive. This monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures (DeBERTa, RoBERTa, BART), spanning six verifier-generator pairs on ProofWriter (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, exact permutation $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}; Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Observation~2: combo gain is significant only when the verifier exceeds the generator. Four of six pairs share the DeBERTa verifier; this dependence reduces effective degrees of freedom, making the permutation test conservative.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$ across three NLI architectures. Gain decreases strongly monotonically (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$); significant gains occur only when the verifier exceeds the generator.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-6mm}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions, confirming that verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain (Observation~2). Note that $\\tau$ was selected on the evaluation set; deployment would require a held-out validation split.\n\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. Results hold across the full capability range: from Mistral-7B (55.88\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86.27\\% SC), SICA delivers no significant gain. $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold logical examples. $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \\textbf{Bold $\\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$);\\enspace{***}\\,$p < 0.001$ (oracle). Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\\ref{app:failure-modes}. $^{4s}$\\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) 75.17 & 72.67 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\\pm 2$\\,pp) confirm 5 equivalent, 5 genuinely different (4 negative), 6 underpowered (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling.\n\n\n",

    "newString": "\\paragraph{Statistical power.}\nMcNemar power for detecting $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp is below 21\\% at $n{=}200$--$600$; our tests are adequately powered only for $|\\Delta| \\geq 5$\\,pp, meaning small true effects may go undetected. The per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \\textsc{False} problems, 69 \\textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered.",
    "originalFile": "\\paragraph{Scale and language.}\nAll models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. The cross-architecture NLI verifier (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo $\\Delta \\leq +1$\\,pp, all $p > 0.5$), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment---NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment. Even on compatible tasks, NLI gain vanishes when the capability gap is large (Qwen3-14B ProofWriter: SC 85.83\\% vs.\\ DeBERTa 55\\%, gap $= -30.83$\\,pp, $\\Delta \\leq +0.50$\\,pp, n.s.), and moderate negative gaps yield small, non-significant gains (LLaMA-3.1-8B: gap $= -17.50$\\,pp, $+0.83$\\,pp, n.s.; \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Scaling the cross-architecture approach---via trained verifiers, code-based symbolic execution, or multi-verifier ensembles---remains unexplored and may extend the escape to stronger generators and broader task types.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical power.}\nThe per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \\textsc{False} problems, 69 \\textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\\approx$ 50--55\\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}\nSICA requires $4.5\\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\\ 17.7\\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\\% of overhead (\\S\\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\\ref{app:limitations-extended}.\n\n\\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.\n",
